I don't mean to be rude, but... well, if I hear my coworkers working through a customer problem, I'll just jump right in with the answer before they've even finished talking, or... before anyone even asked me for help. I just kind of blurt it out.\n\nDoctor: Do you also often lose things you need for tasks, like keys, paperwork, or your phone?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I guess everyone misplaces their phone once in a while, but... no, I usually know where my stuff is. I've always got a specific spot for my keys at home, and at work, my papers and headset just stay right there on my desk. So I wouldn't say losing things is really an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often leave your seat when you’re expected to stay seated?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I mean, with my job, I'm on the phone a lot, so I pretty much have to stay glued to my headset. I might bounce my leg like I mentioned earlier, but... no, I don't actually get up and walk around when I'm supposed to be working. I just stay put.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when your mood was unusually high, expansive, or irritable for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say I have. I mean, sure, I get a little irritable if it's been a long day on the phones, but... no, nothing like being unusually high or, you know, feeling like that for days at a time. My mood is pretty steady, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you often make careless mistakes because you miss details?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do actually. At work, I’ll... I'll think I’ve completely finished a customer ticket, and then later on I notice I typed the wrong account number. Or maybe I skipped a required box on the screen. It's usually just missing one little instruction, but it makes the whole thing come back to me to fix. So, yeah, that happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or driven to keep moving even when you’re expected to stay still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do get that feeling. If I have to sit still through a training video for work, or maybe a slow appointment, I start to feel kind of boxed in. It's like I really need to stand up and stretch, or just... do something with my hands. I don't always get up, like I said before, but that restless feeling is definitely there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often get distracted by noises or other things happening around you when you’re trying to focus?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Working from home or even in the office, there's just so much going on. I'll have the chat window open for a customer, the CRM system pulled up, and then our team messages going off. If I hear one beep from a message, or if someone's talking nearby, it just... it pulls me right off track. I'll completely lose my place with the customer I was helping and have to figure out where we left off.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you can’t stay seated, even when you’re expected to?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I feel that restless feeling I mentioned a minute ago, but I don't actually get up. I know I'm supposed to stay in my seat when I'm on the clock or in a meeting, so I just stay put. I might tap my foot or fidget, but I don't feel like I absolutely can't stay seated.\n\nDoctor: Do you often leave your seat when you’re expected to stay seated?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. Like I said just a second ago, I know I need to stay in my chair when I'm working or in a meeting. I might feel a bit antsy, but I don't actually get up and walk away when I'm supposed to be sitting.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you need to run about or climb on things when you’re supposed to stay calm or still?\n\nPatient: No, I don't run around or climb on things. I'm 57, so that's not really something I do. But... I do get this feeling sometimes, like if I have to sit still through a long training video for work, or if things are just really slow. I start feeling kind of boxed in, you know? Like I just need to stand up, or stretch my legs, or at least do something with my hands because just sitting there doing nothing makes me feel too restless.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk too much?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. So I wouldn't say losing things is really an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often leave your seat when you’re expected to stay seated?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I mean, with my job, I'm on the phone a lot, so I pretty much have to stay glued to my headset. I might bounce my leg like I mentioned earlier, but... no, I don't actually get up and walk around when I'm supposed to be working. I just stay put.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when your mood was unusually high, expansive, or irritable for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say I have. I mean, sure, I get a little irritable if it's been a long day on the phones, but... no, nothing like being unusually high or, you know, feeling like that for days at a time. My mood is pretty steady, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you often make careless mistakes because you miss details?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do actually. At work, I’ll... I'll think I’ve completely finished a customer ticket, and then later on I notice I typed the wrong account number. Or maybe I skipped a required box on the screen. It's usually just missing one little instruction, but it makes the whole thing come back to me to fix. So, yeah, that happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or driven to keep moving even when you’re expected to stay still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do get that feeling. If I have to sit still through a training video for work, or maybe a slow appointment, I start to feel kind of boxed in. It's like I really need to stand up and stretch, or just... do something with my hands. I don't always get up, like I said before, but that restless feeling is definitely there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often get distracted by noises or other things happening around you when you’re trying to focus?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Working from home or even in the office, there's just so much going on. I'll have the chat window open for a customer, the CRM system pulled up, and then our team messages going off. If I hear one beep from a message, or if someone's talking nearby, it just... it pulls me right off track. I'll completely lose my place with the customer I was helping and have to figure out where we left off.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you can’t stay seated, even when you’re expected to?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I feel that restless feeling I mentioned a minute ago, but I don't actually get up. I know I'm supposed to stay in my seat when I'm on the clock or in a meeting, so I just stay put. I might tap my foot or fidget, but I don't feel like I absolutely can't stay seated.\n\nDoctor: Do you often leave your seat when you’re expected to stay seated?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. Like I said just a second ago, I know I need to stay in my chair when I'm working or in a meeting. I might feel a bit antsy, but I don't actually get up and walk away when I'm supposed to be sitting.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you need to run about or climb on things when you’re supposed to stay calm or still?\n\nPatient: No, I don't run around or climb on things. I'm 57, so that's not really something I do. But... I do get this feeling sometimes, like if I have to sit still through a long training video for work, or if things are just really slow. I start feeling kind of boxed in, you know? Like I just need to stand up, or stretch my legs, or at least do something with my hands because just sitting there doing nothing makes me feel too restless.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk too much?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I talk to customers all day for my job, but I just stick to the script and answer their questions. I don't really go off on tangents or talk people's ears off in my regular life, either. I pretty much just say what I need to say.\n\nDoctor: Before age 15, did you often get into trouble for breaking rules or acting aggressively, like fighting, stealing, lying, or destroying things?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't do any of that. I was a pretty normal kid, really. I wasn't getting into fights or stealing things or anything like that. I mostly just stayed out of trouble.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you often have trouble paying attention in school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. They'd say I talked out of turn, and that I constantly had to be reminded to just stay with my work. So, yeah, paying attention was definitely an issue back then.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often fidget, squirm, or have trouble sitting still when you were supposed to be quiet or seated?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I had any more trouble sitting still than the next kid.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you often have trouble waiting your turn?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't remember having any real issues with waiting my turn or anything like that back then.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, sure, I get a little irritable if it's been a long day on the phones, but... no, nothing like being unusually high or, you know, feeling like that for days at a time. My mood is pretty steady, really.\n\nDoctor: Do you often make careless mistakes because you miss details?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do actually. At work, I’ll... I'll think I’ve completely finished a customer ticket, and then later on I notice I typed the wrong account number. Or maybe I skipped a required box on the screen. It's usually just missing one little instruction, but it makes the whole thing come back to me to fix. So, yeah, that happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or driven to keep moving even when you’re expected to stay still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do get that feeling. If I have to sit still through a training video for work, or maybe a slow appointment, I start to feel kind of boxed in. It's like I really need to stand up and stretch, or just... do something with my hands. I don't always get up, like I said before, but that restless feeling is definitely there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often get distracted by noises or other things happening around you when you’re trying to focus?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Working from home or even in the office, there's just so much going on. I'll have the chat window open for a customer, the CRM system pulled up, and then our team messages going off. If I hear one beep from a message, or if someone's talking nearby, it just... it pulls me right off track. I'll completely lose my place with the customer I was helping and have to figure out where we left off.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you can’t stay seated, even when you’re expected to?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I feel that restless feeling I mentioned a minute ago, but I don't actually get up. I know I'm supposed to stay in my seat when I'm on the clock or in a meeting, so I just stay put. I might tap my foot or fidget, but I don't feel like I absolutely can't stay seated.\n\nDoctor: Do you often leave your seat when you’re expected to stay seated?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. Like I said just a second ago, I know I need to stay in my chair when I'm working or in a meeting. I might feel a bit antsy, but I don't actually get up and walk away when I'm supposed to be sitting.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you need to run about or climb on things when you’re supposed to stay calm or still?\n\nPatient: No, I don't run around or climb on things. I'm 57, so that's not really something I do. But... I do get this feeling sometimes, like if I have to sit still through a long training video for work, or if things are just really slow. I start feeling kind of boxed in, you know? Like I just need to stand up, or stretch my legs, or at least do something with my hands because just sitting there doing nothing makes me feel too restless.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk too much?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I talk to customers all day for my job, but I just stick to the script and answer their questions. I don't really go off on tangents or talk people's ears off in my regular life, either. I pretty much just say what I need to say.\n\nDoctor: Before age 15, did you often get into trouble for breaking rules or acting aggressively, like fighting, stealing, lying, or destroying things?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't do any of that. I was a pretty normal kid, really. I wasn't getting into fights or stealing things or anything like that. I mostly just stayed out of trouble.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you often have trouble paying attention in school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. Looking back, I realize this has been around long before any recent job changes. Back in grade school, my teachers used to write on my report cards that I was bright, but careless. They'd say I talked out of turn, and that I constantly had to be reminded to just stay with my work. So, yeah, paying attention was definitely an issue back then.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you often fidget, squirm, or have trouble sitting still when you were supposed to be quiet or seated?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't remember being particularly squirmy or anything. I don't think I had any more trouble sitting still than the next kid.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you often have trouble waiting your turn?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't remember having any real issues with waiting my turn or anything like that back then.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you often have trouble staying seated when you were expected to?\n\nPatient: No, I don't remember that being a problem. I mean, I stayed in my desk just fine. My mind might have been wandering off, but I wasn't getting up and walking around the classroom when I wasn't supposed to."
